Chapter 255: Calmly Considering Actions

To be honest, at the beginning, Ji Yuan did not think that Lu Chengfeng would undergo such a big change just because he had gone to the underworld to see his deceased parents.

This was because, in Ji Yuan’s view, although the former Lu Chengfeng was polite and smooth in his social dealings, he was still just an ordinary person in the end. He would not look up to Lu Chengfeng because of his previous achievements in the martial arts world, nor would he look down on him because he had been crushed by the cruelty of reality. He could only respond out of sympathy and care for his old friend.

But the human heart was the most inscrutable of all, both for good and for ill. After returning from the underworld of Desheng Prefecture, Lu Chengfeng was no longer an ordinary person.

Ji Yuan could clearly feel that Warrior Lu had become a true warrior, a hero that even he, who was considered an immortal or even a god in the eyes of ordinary people, could recognize. Well, perhaps other practitioners might not recognize him as a hero, but Ji Yuan certainly could.

He turned around from the courtyard entrance, closing the gate once more and looking back at the courtyard. The branches and leaves of the jujube tree were still swaying with the breeze. Although they were moving, they seemed even more peaceful.

“It seems you also see it!”

That jujube was not given by Ji Yuan, but rather by the jujube tree itself.

“Buzz…”

The Green Vine Sword, which had been leaning against the jujube tree, also let out a soft sound, revealing its presence, as if it could understand what its master was thinking with just Ji Yuan’s half-sentence.

“Ah, I know, you recognize it too.”

Ji Yuan smiled and went back to the stone table. He picked up the jade slip and continued to read its contents. He also felt some unique thoughts revolving around Lu Chengfeng’s sudden visit.

There was a saying: destiny had its own unseen plans.1 Ji Yuan believed that this saying was by no means purely constrained to destiny; in fact, it was most likely about the laws of all things in nature, as well as the law of nature itself. However, it could also be taken to mean that when there was a causal relationship, then things would indeed have a corresponding effect.

Now was the great turning point of Lu Shanjun’s life, a time of complete transformation. According to his analogy of the dragons and flood dragons, it would take at least five years at the fastest, and ten years at the slowest, for the tiger spirit to be completely reborn. He believed that his transformation would be more natural at that time. After all, most aquatic spirits could transform into their human forms soon after successfully being reborn as flood dragons, and there were even many who could transform before they were reborn into flood dragons.

This moment also coincided with a turning point for Lu Chengfeng himself, who had achieved a breakthrough. It could only be concluded that this was a wonderful twist of fate.2 If one truly looked into it, the reason why Lu Shanjun and those nine people had made a covenant was that, in addition to the role Ji Yuan played, Lu Chengfeng was undoubtedly the initial link.

Ji Yuan was very curious as to what the tiger would transform into. After all, he had not heard of any such precedent up until now. This kind of transformation was probably related to his retelling of “Xiao Yaoyou.” He was now even more curious about how Lu Shanjun would fulfill his covenant when the time came.

At present, those nine young warriors might not even remember their original agreement with the tiger. Even Lu Chengfeng, who had just left, seemed to follow these expectations. Or, at the very least, he hadn’t mentioned it to Ji Yuan.

Over the years, Ji Yuan had visited the large stone platform on Niu Kui Mountain several times. He had lectured to Lu Shanjun several times with his increasingly proficient manifestation method of his inner world. Although Lu Shanjun had never mentioned the covenant, Ji Yuan knew him much better than the nine young warriors did. He knew that his cheap disciple valued his word, and kept their agreement in mind.

After reading the jade slip for a while, Ji Yuan stopped again. Lu Chengfeng’s visit made him curious.

Thus, setting aside Du Heng and Lu Chengfeng, he used the names of the other seven warriors and his memories to make a hexagram. After a while, Ji Yuan frowned.

He didn’t know their dates of birth, plus it had been so many years: under such limited conditions, with Ji Yuan’s skills in divination, this hexagram could only be considered ambiguous, roughly determining a single direction.

But even so, he could still feel a vague sense of good or bad.

‘Forget it, I’d better not get involved. This is a ‘surprise’ that belongs between them and Shanjun.’

. . .

Five days later, in front of the Lu family’s house in Cloud Pavilion, Yuchang County, Lu Chengfeng got off of his yellow horse, brushed the dust off his body, took off the bamboo hat on his head, and went straight to find his elder brother, regardless of his fatigue and sleepiness.

Half an hour later, in the house next to the back warehouse at home, Lu Chengfeng found his brother hard at work.

As soon as he saw Lu Chengfeng, Lu Chengyun frowned.

“Where have you been for so many days? Why didn’t you let the servants follow you? Do you need me to explain to you again just how bad the situation is in our Lu family these past two years?”

Lu Chengfeng didn’t talk back, just smiled. He took out a ball of carefully wrapped cloth from his arms, untied it to reveal a fiery red jujube.

“Brother, I brought you a rare jujube fruit, a gift from a master. It is absolutely something extraordinary.”

In fact, Lu Chengfeng knew very well that this jujube was extraordinary. He had studied it more than once on the way back. Whether it is the constant warmth or the occasional flickers of firelight in the middle of the night, all of it fully contributed to the magic of this fruit.

“What is this? A jujube? One that looks like this, at this time of year?”

Lu Chengyun was also attracted by the fire jujube. Out of curiosity, he couldn’t help but grab the jujube to take a closer look, but as soon as he touched it, he felt a fire in his hands.

“Ah!”

Lu Chengyun looked at his Lu Chengfeng in amazement, and seemed to realize that what his younger brother just said was true. After tossing it in his hands for a while, he handed it back to Lu Chengfeng, but the latter blocked his hand as soon as he held it out.

“I brought it back for you. I don’t know if it will go bad after this long. Brother, it would be best to eat it as soon as possible.”

After saying this, Lu Chengfeng turned around and left, not wanting to explain too much.

Lu Chengyun looked at the jujube fruit. The fruit in his hand had a strong allure. Just looking at it made him feel like salivating.

“Chengfeng, why don’t you…”

“I don’t need it anymore!”

Lu Chengfeng stood still, turning to look at his brother. He refused even before the other party finished speaking, then left without looking back.

“Where are you going?”

“‘I’m going to sleep first, then get up to practice.”

Lu Chengfeng’s silhouette had disappeared at the door of the house, but his voice came back from outside.

It was just a few words, but the tone of his voice made Lu Chengyun inexplicably feel that his younger brother seemed to be a little different.

. . .

It was the summer rainy season3 in Ji Province, and it was currently raining on this day.

Ji Yuan did not go into the yard, but instead picked up a chair and sat under the eaves in front of the house, reading the letter in his hand.

Communication in this era of Dazhen was not convenient. Sometimes no letters for a long period of time did not necessarily mean that no one had written. For example, at the current moment, when Ji Yuan finally received his mail, it had come as a whole bundle. Who knew which post office had suffered some issues? It was impossible to check these matters in real time. The letters had probably accumulated for a while before someone realized the problem and sent them out.

In his previous life, he had encountered many similar situations with express deliveries, not to mention in this new era. As long as it wasn’t a military letter, or something someone had come to deliver in person, its arrival was not guaranteed.

Reading letters was easier than reading books. The writer was talking to Ji Yuan, and there was a hint of their aura when he touched the paper, which to a certain extent allowed Ji Yuan to read the letter without being affected to a certain extent by his own poor eyesight.

There was a letter from Du Heng, which mentioned Lu Chengfeng, and said that Lu Chengfeng had grown indulgent due to great changes in his family. He couldn’t help but mention Mr. Ji to him, incentivizing Lu Chengfeng to come to Ji Yuan, so he wrote a letter to apologize.

There was also a letter from Bing Province. The author of the letter was Qin Zizhou, who had changed a lot. He wrote about his current cultivation practice, and mentioned that he had studied Taoist scriptures related to astronomy with two Taoist priests in Cloud Mountain Temple. Being in harmony with the star map chart and the banner in the temple was very helpful to him. He could even occasionally draw a traces of spiritual power from the stars at night, such that he could finally embark on his true path of cultivation.

In the first half of his cultivation practice, things were quite normal. In the second half, Qin Zizhou seemed puzzled and amused, asking Ji Yuan several questions. The general idea of his words was that Taoist Qingsong was currently growing more and more robust thanks to the spiritual energy that Ji Yuan had once guided into him, and Qin Zizhou’s own cultivation. As a result he had become even more obsessed with food and his passions, and less concerned with the consequences. Was this a good or bad development?

Ji Yuan knew very well what Taoist Qingsong’s so-called “passions” were, but what could he say?

The remaining two letters were all sent from Wan Province. The first one was signed Yin Zhaoxian. There was nothing special about it. Apart from chatting about his current situation and daily life, it also served as a belated New Year’s greeting letter. Ji Yuan watched the spring rain outside as he read it, thinking that he should pay the Yin family an early New Year’s visit this year.

The handwriting of the second letter was rather strange. It looked rough and awkward. Ji Yuan could tell at a glance that it had been written by Yin Qing with his left hand. There was not even a signature. Ji Yuan’s expression grew a little more serious.

The letter mentioned that Emperor Yuan De’s health was declining. He had begun to ignore government affairs, and even relinquished power to Prince Wu in court to suppress the other princes.

But some of the things that happened at Assembly gathering at that time made the old emperor firmly believe that there must be a path to immortality somewhere in the world, so he was even more relentless in seeking medicine and supernatural means during this period of time.

Even Yin Qing vaguely mentioned that “Prince Wu wishes not only to win over the ministers, but also the scholars.”

Ji Yuan put down the letter, looked at the drizzle outside, and thought for a long time.

The scholars mentioned naturally referred to Yin Zhaoxian. Prince Wu even tried to win over a governor like Yin Zhaoxian, who was far away in Wan Province. Even without considering the politics of the capital, was clear that there were indeed undercurrents surging in the imperial court of Dazhen.

Yin Zhaoxian wouldn’t write about this kind of thing in a latter, but Yin Qing specifically made sure to inform him, which was quite interesting.

The current most legitimate prince in line to take the throne once the old emperor died was naturally Prince Wu, but Prince Jin was no pushover either. He was more powerful than his brother in terms of means. Any conflict between them would likely end in death.

Unfortunately, Ji Yuan had no intention of intervening in this battle for the throne. However, he could certainly just go over there and take a look.

He had been cultivating at home for almost two years, and it was time for him to travel. He also wanted to find the storyteller Wang Li, and listen to “The White Deer’s Fate” now that it had been published. If the tale turned out okay, he might go give him some new stories.

Ji Yuan sat for a while, then went back to his room to grind ink and write a letter to Wan Province. He did not reply to any of the other letters. Then he locked the inner and outer doors to the small pavilion, and left.

冥冥之中 (míngmíngzhīzhōng) the underworld of spirits/mysteriously and inexorably, 自有 (zìyǒu): one’s own, 定数 (dìngshù): constant/destiny. There are several different ways one could translate this (“one’s own destiny is mysterious and unseen,” “the underworld has its own destiny,” “one never knows one’s own destiny,” etc.) all with the same general meaning but slightly different connotations.

Cheesy pun on the part of the author with JY’s name. Lit. “wonderful way of fate” could also say “wonderful path of Yuan,” because JY set him up for success

Lit. “plum rains season,” prolonged rainfall during the summer months

Chapter 256: The Storyteller

Wang Li staggered along the cold streets of the city, wearing a grey robe. Dawn had only just broken, so there were very few passers-by.

“Haah… hooh…”

Wang Li felt a chill in the early morning wind, and he shivered.

One would feel hot at the very start of getting drunk, but it wouldn’t take long afterwards to feel cold, especially on the street in the early morning after the spring rain.

He shook the wine jug in his hand, and seeing that there was no more wine in it, Wang Li threw it away.

The wine jug made a parabolic curve in the air and landed on the street not too far away.

“Crash…”

The jug completely shattered with a crisp sound, Wang Li continued to stumble away.

Ji Yuan looked down at the broken pottery jug that had fallen at his feet, then to Wang Li, who was staggering away, and couldn’t help but frown. This person’s current state was a little different than what he had imagined.

“Wan’er… Wan’er…”

Wang Li murmured, tightening his clothes and walking towards his residence. He was no longer in the capital now, but rather in Chengsu Prefecture, east of the section of the Tongtian River that passed by the capital, a territory which belonged to You Province.

Just like every time Wang Li went to a new place, he lived in someone’s private residence: renting a side room from the owner was much more cost-effective than living in an inn for a long time.

“Ah…hooh… Hey, Mr. Wang, did you just come back?”

The owner of the house exited his residence, stretching his waist, and greeted Wang Li, who was still a little shaky.

“Haha, good morning…”

Wang Li swayed and bowed crookedly with cupped hands. He walked to his side room, pushed open the door, and stumbled, grabbing the doorknob as he fell in. He held the doorknob so tightly that he didn’t fall all the way down.

“Oh, Mr. Wang, how much wine did you drink?!”

The owner hurried over to help him, and helped Wang Li down onto the bed.

“Thank you, thank you…”

Wang Li fell on the bed and cupped his hands in a daze, then pulled the quilt halfway over himself. He soon began to snore.

“Tsk tsk tsk tsk…”

The owner shook his head, exited the room, and closed the door.

The owner’s wife in the main hall behind the side room had gotten dressed, and soon came out. When she saw the owner, she asked in a low voice.

“Did he drink too much again?”

“Ah… He drank a lot and then fell asleep immediately.”

“Mr. Wang was such a good man when he first came, but now he is like this all the time.”

“We can’t control this, as long as he pays the rent.”

The two whispered, then went back to wash up.

It wasn’t until the sun began to set that Wang Li, who was still lying on the bed, woke up from his sleep. He rubbed his temples, sat up from the bed, and looked around in a daze. It took a while for him to gradually wake up.

Wang Li’s face was a little pale. He lifted the quilt, got out of the bed, and opened the door. The afterglow of the setting sun pierced his eyes. Smoke was already rising from the kitchen beside him.

“Mr. Wang, are you awake?”

The owner’s wife, who was hanging clothes to dry, greeted Wang Li with a smile when she saw him come out.

“Mrs. Liu, what time is it now?”

“I didn’t check the sundial, but it must be almost the You hour.1 You got up just in time. We will be eating fish today…”

“It’s the You hour? I have to leave quickly…”

When Wang Li heard the time, he slapped his forehead, then went back into the house in a hurry to pack his things. He took his paper fan, his attention-stick, his books… all of it went into a bundle, then he hurried out again with his bundle.

“Hey… Mr. Wang, don’t you want to eat?”

“No, no, I’m in a rush!”

Wang Li hurriedly left the yard and rushed out. Today, he was headed to a big restaurant to tell stories. A wealthy family had booked the entire restaurant for a banquet, inviting guests to congratulate their children on their success in the imperial examination, and attaining the qualification to become officials.

It was already the You hour, and the banquet might have already started. If Wang Li, the storyteller, did not show up after the banquet had begun, it would be considered a breach of contract. Not only would he not be able to get paid, but he would also have to return their deposit, which they had already spent.

The street was already crowded with people coming and going. Wang Li was in a hurry. He didn’t even bother to wipe away his sweat, he simply kept trotting forward. When he hurriedly reached the outside of Zhongtai Building on the bustling street, he was already out of breath.

“Haah…haah…haah…haah…”

Someone was welcoming the guests outside of the Zhongtai building at this moment. Respectable guests of all ages were entering the building, and they all bowed as soon as they arrived at the entrance.

“Master Gao, congratulations!”

“Young Master Gao has a bright future!”

“Hahahaha… Thank you, thank you, please come in!”

“Mr. Gao is very lucky, your son has passed the imperial examination! From now on he will be an official like us!”

“Ahahahahahaha…”

“Master Gao, Young Master Gao, you have to take care of us common people in the future!”

“Hahaha, Master Wang, if you’re a common person, wouldn’t my Gao family be considered street vendors? Hahahaha… please come in!”

“Uncle Wang, you are too polite!”

“Hahahahaha…”

……

The Gao family was outside, welcoming the guests with smiles on their faces, constantly bowing and returning the greetings without showing any tiredness. They looked even happier than newlyweds.

Wang Li leaned against a wall not too far away, breathing heavily. Seeing the situation, he couldn’t help but heave a sigh of relief. It seemed that the banquet had not yet started.

After easing his breath for a while, Wang Li looked himself up and down, tidied up his clothes, wiped the sweat on his face and neck with the cloth bundle on his back, then resumed his gentle pace and walked towards Zhongtai Building.

Before he got close, Wang Li had already bowed and congratulated with a smile.

“Master Gao, Young Master Gao, congratulations, congratulations. I am not too late, am I?”

Master Gao was a very spirited old man in brocade robes, and Young Master Gao was also tall and elegant. The two of them politely returned the greetings.

“Mr. Wang you came at just the right time. The banquet has not yet started. I have already ordered the food to be prepared for you in advance. Someone will direct you after you enter!”

“Okay, thank you Master Gao!”

Wang Li bowed again and hurried into the Zhongtai Building. He was really hungry and thirsty by now, and Master Gao’s preparations were exactly what he wanted.

As a storyteller, he was tasked with bringing joy to the guests at the banquet. The others could listen while eating, but Wang Li couldn’t talk and eat at the same time. Considering that storytelling was also a physical job, he naturally had to eat in advance.

After entering the restaurant, a servant took Wang Li to a private room on the second floor. There was a large table of dishes ready, but Wang Li was not the only one eating. There were also some women with zithers and harps, who had already picked up their chopsticks. Obviously, Wang Li was still a little late.

“Mr. Wang, please eat here.”

“Okay, thank you.”

The servant didn’t say anything more and left to complete his own business.

Wang Li greeted everyone in the room with a little embarrassment, and then he sat down and started eating. Because he was hungry and thirsty, he naturally ate and drank heartily, which made the few women around him laugh lightly.

After a while, the number of guests gathering towards the Zhongtai Building gradually decreased. Master Gao had planned to finally go into the restaurant, seeing as his cheek muscles were sore from laughing and smiling for such a long time, while Young Master Gao had already gone in earlier.

“Master Gao, congratulations, congratulations, three times congratulations to your son on his success in the imperial examination!”

A calm but powerful voice rang out, which made Master Gao and his servants, who were just about to go inside, turn their heads subconsciously to look around. They found a gentleman in a green robe standing outside. Aside from a black jade hairpin on his head, he did not seem to have any jade pendants or other accessories on his body. He looked simple, but had a powerful bearing, and he bowed with cupped hands to congratulate them, a smile on his face.

“Uh… who might you be?”

Master Gao asked in confusion as he returned the greeting.

“Oh, my name is Ji Yuan. I wished to have a meal at Zhongtai Building, but when I saw that the Gao family had booked the restaurant to celebrate the Gao family’s son’s success in the imperial examination, I wished to congratulate him as well. Congratulations!”

Ji Yuan pointed to the red paper sign leaning against the outside of the building, which said that the Gao family had booked the restaurant for a day, welcoming guests to congratulate the Gao family’s son on his success in the imperial examination. It apologized to the other customers that would have to come back another day.

Master Gao suddenly understood and bowed in return.

“Oh, thank you for your congratulations, Sir!”

Ji Yuan nodded, let down his cupped hands, and waved his hand, then turned to walk towards the other restaurants and wine shops along the street.

“Ah, Sir, please stay! If you don’t mind, please come upstairs and join us at the banquet.”

When Mr. Gao saw Mr. Ji’s extraordinary demeanor, he couldn’t help but greet him. It was just a meal, after all.

Ji Yuan turned around, pretended to think for a moment, then smiled and bowed again to express his gratitude.

“I am new to Bao Lane, and I heard that the food at Zhongtai Building is delicious. In that case, thank you, Master Gao!”

“You’re welcome, Sir. Please come in!”

Master Gao welcomed Ji Yuan and the two entered the building together.

Because he did not know Ji Yuan before, although it was a happy occasion to meet such a gentleman with a great bearing and have him congratulate him, it was of course impossible to arrange a seat for him that was too crowded, so he directed him to the second floor, slightly outside the hall.

Ji Yuan didn’t mind, and he had no right to disdain a free meal. The people at this table were obviously not familiar with each other. After chatting for a while, as the hot and fragrant dishes came up, everyone seemed to get along well.

“Everyone, my Gao family is blessed with a bright future and good fortune. We are also fortunate to have invited a famous storyteller from the capital. Now, let us invite Mr. Wang Li to tell us a story to liven up the banquet!”

“Ah, that’s great!”

“A famous person from the capital?”

“Tell us some stories we haven’t heard before.”

“Exactly!”

“Wang Li? I think I’ve heard of him in the capital. It is said that he can perform ventriloquism, and he’s quite good at it!”

“Really?”

“Of course!”

…

Ji Yuan could hear the guests’ chattering, full of anticipation. In front of them, a servant carried a table to an open space in the center of the building. Atop it were the attention-stick and several other objects. Someone else carried two screens up to the center, forming two semicircles in the front and back to conceal the table.

Wang Li slowly came out of the private side room, which served as a preparation room, and walked behind the screens, holding a white paper fan. Several women carrying zithers and lutes also walked out and came to either side, preparing themselves.

“Dear guests, today is a joyous occasion for the Gao family. I, Wang Li, have brought you a new story that you have never heard before. This story is quite extraordinary; it was imparted upon me in a dream by an immortal. This story is called ‘The White Deer’s Fate’!”

Wang Li’s opening remarks immediately raised everyone’s interest even higher. Even Ji Yuan put down his chopsticks, intrigued. He, too, wanted to hear Wang Li’s finished story.

T/n: I aspire to be like JY, just showing up at fancy parties looking nice and getting free food.

Unfortunately I won’t be able to update much this week, sorry. See you all on Monday!

酉 (yǒu) hour = 5-7 PM

Chapter 257: The Goddess In White

[T/n: Note on chapter title]1

With a full stomach, having rested for a while, and having slept all day, Wang Li was now in good spirits, and his speech was full of energy.

This “White Deer’s Fate” was Wang Li’s own completely exclusive story, and he had only told it on private occasions in the capital and in some prefectures. This was not the kind of story that most people were familiar with, and he could be sure that no other storyteller had mixed in with the guests here to “steal his story.”

Therefore, this story was something that the other storytellers would never hear. The response every time the story was told was not bad. In addition, this was indeed a “dream from an immortal,” so Wang Li at this moment was feeling extraordinarily confident.

“Shuaa…”

The paper fan in his hand opened, and the calm Wang Li picked up his attention-stick and slapped it heavily on the table.

“Crack!”

After the crisp sound, the woman beside him plucked the instrument in her hand. The chords of the zither and side plucked the hand-made musical instrument, and the chords of the zither and pipa rang out. In the moment, Wang Li’s voice left a slightly hazy feeling.

“It is said that during the Shuncheng dynasty,2 in a remote county town of our Dazhen, there was a scholar surnamed Zhou…”

Wang Li was indeed very good at storytelling. The combination of chord music and his outstanding ventriloquism created an immersive feeling for the audience. He imitated the scholar’s spirit, the voices of the elders of the family, and even the charming laughter of the women.

The guests originally thought it was a story about rushing to take the imperial examinations to seek fame. After all, at present, the son of the Gao family had just passed the imperial examination, so this kind of story would be quite suitable for the occasion. However, as the story progressed, it seemed as though sequential layers of thin gauze curtains were drawn back, and the mythological undercurrents of the story began to gradually reveal themselves.

Ji Yuan was listening attentively. Occasionally he looked around, distracted, and found that all the guests, both nearby and far away, were all serious and invested. Almost no one touched their chopsticks or drank.

He had also heard some storytellers tell their stories before. Some of them were very good — at least, they could attract Ji Yuan’s attention, but storytellers as skilled as Wang Li were indeed rare.

From an objective standpoint, Wang Li’s current skills were more sophisticated now than they were when he was at the New Year’s Eve banquet at Prince Jin’s palace. Obviously, in addition to Ji Yuan’s technique of conveying information through objects, Wang Li’s own talent was the key to his progress.

“Wuuhoooo…”

The strange and empty sound of a deer’s call came from behind the screen. The guests in the room clutched their trouser legs and clenched their fists. Many people felt their scalps grow numb, and goosebumps spread on their bodies. They all recognized this monstrous cry.

Wang Li’s imitation of the deer’s cry made it feel even more as though there was no human behind the screen, but rather a demon deer.

“Slam…”

“Slam…”

“Slam…”

Several windows were blown open by the wind.

“Whoooooo… Whooooo…”

A cool night breeze blew in through the windows on the second floor of the Zhongtai Building, making everyone feel even colder.

The servants of the Gao family hurriedly closed the windows again, but the emotions brought up by the story did not subsisde.

If it weren’t for the shadows cast by a lamp behind the screen, which meant that the audience could still see the gentleman holding a paper fan, some people might even have stood up in fright.

Ji Yuan’s eyes were indifferent. Wang Li spoke very well, but the “plot” seemed to have changed. His gaze swept towards the window.

As a gust of cool wind blew past, a woman in white with light makeup appeared on the originally vacant seat at a table in the corner, staring in the direction of the screen.

‘Interesting. There is no divine light visible, but there is a lingering scent of incense. Could she be a goddess? ’

Ji Yuan remained silent, but a smile appeared at the corner of his mouth.

“Crack!”

The attention-stick smacked down and woke everyone up.

“If you want to know what happened next, we shall listen to the continuation after a cup of tea!”

After Wang Li spoke, many guests breathed a sigh of relief. Patriarch Gao immediately stood up and said.

“Everyone, let us eat and drink first. The new dishes have not been touched yet. Come, come, everyone, let’s eat, let’s eat!”

Most of the guests were so engrossed in listening now that they didn’t spare a thought to eating. Now that the storytelling had ended, everyone resumed eating and drinking, but many of them continued to discuss the story as they ate.

Ji Yuan did the same. He picked up a piece of the freshly-served white-cut chicken3 over glutinous rice, dipped the edge in the dipping sauce, and put it into his mouth to savor the taste.

“Oh, this famous storyteller from the capital is really extraordinary!”

“Yes, yes, I got goosebumps all over just now.”

“You too? Me too!”

“Oh, what a coincidence.”

“Mr. Wang’s ventriloquism is really good. I have seen ventriloquists once or twice before, but none of them were as good as he is.”

“Yes, and there is also the matter of this “White Deer’s Fate.” This story is so good but I haven’t heard it before. Is it really Mr. Wang’s own creation?”

Hearing this, a young man at Ji Yuan’s table seemed very excited.

“He said it was imparted to him by a god in a dream. I also heard that Mr. Wang Li travels the world looking for all kinds of magical stories. He is quite a strange man.”

Ji Yuan just continued eating the food, echoing the words of the people chatting around him. From time to time, he glanced at the other side of the screen. Based on the shadow cast by the lamp from inside the shades, Wang Li was also taking this opportunity to drink tea to quench his thirst. One of the woman who had just played the pipa stood up and fanned him with a paper fan.

Behind the screens, Wang Li also tried to rest at this moment. After drinking tea, he closed his eyes and relaxed. After about a quarter of an hour, he opened his eyes and bowed with cupped hands to the woman beside him.

“Thank you, young lady. You should take a rest. You still have to play the pipa later.”

“Okay!”

The woman put down her fan and walked out of the screen, sitting back in her seat. Her companions immediately started chatting with her, pointing at the screen and laughing.

As time passed, Ji Yuan could clearly feel that the guests were not paying attention to the dishes, because Wang Li was about to start speaking again. Looking at the woman in white who had just arrived, it seemed she was in a similar state.

Ji Yuan was the only one at his table who continued eating. The dishes in Zhongtai Building were delicious. He had mostly eaten simple meals in recent years, and occasionally changing his accustomed flavors was really intoxicating.

“Crack!”

The attention-stick cracked. Wang Li stood up and tossed aside the paper fan, and the full-bodied voice sounded again. Now no one was eating anymore, and everyone listened intently.

“Last time, we spoke of Miss Bai, who turned out to be a white deer that had become a spirit. She appeared in front of Scholar Zhou in the form of young woman…”

Wang Li began to tell the story again. Because the story at this time had progressed to a certain point, it began to have fewer details about the scholar studying hard to seek an official position, and there began to be many more mythological details and plots of mysterious love.

During this part of the tale, there were ghosts, demons, and some dangers. Some plots were designed by Wang Li to set a pacing of ups and downs for the story.

Ji Yuan had to sigh again in his heart: Wang Li was truly a talent. It was obvious that Zhou Shusheng, whose yang energy was weak and had been touched by evil spirits, had encountered a ghost on the road at night, but Wang Li added details and changed it to make it become a thrilling and magical story, with evil ghosts and magicians appearing together, and the Lady White Deer saving her husband…

Even Ji Yuan felt the same sense of fun that the derived from watching dramas in his previous life. The guests were already fascinated.

In the end, when he described how the white deer followed her old husband down into the underworld to seek an audience with the City God, and was detained an had her soul extracted, most of the female guests were already in tears.

“Ever since then, the Lady White Deer has sequestered herself in the underworld, living with Scholar Zhou. Every year, on the anniversary of Scholar Zhou’s death, the Lady White Deer is whipped in the underworld. True love is the hardest thing to find in this world, and this love between a human and demon who shall forever be together is truly bittersweet…”

When Wang Li said this, he gently pressed on the attention-stick, making a dull sound.

“Wuuwoooo…”

The faint sound of deer crying came from behind the screen again.

The guests around him still felt like they were being electrified when they heard it, but there was significantly less fear, and the emotional weight was even heavier.

Many people subconsciously rubbed their arms and thighs. They had gotten a lot of goosebumps at the end of this story.

“Clap clap clap…”

“Wonderful!”

“Well said, well spoken!”

“Clap clap clap…”

“So wonderful, extremely wonderful!”

“Mr. Wang is truly a great talent!”

“Yes, it was really well-told.”

…

The full audience applauded and praised the storyteller Wang Li for his superb skills.

There were four chapters of this story in total, and the full tale took nearly two hours to tell. When he had finished, the two screens were carried away by the servants, revealing the sweating Wang Li inside.

Patriarch Gao and Young Master Gao also stood up together and bowed with cupped hands in thanks.

“Thank you Mr. Wang for your midnight wit. Thank you, sir.”

Wang Li hurriedly got up and returned the bow, speaking humbly.

Ji Yuan also applauded, but his attention was naturally more focused on the window. He saw the woman staring at Wang Li with cold eyes, waving her long sleeves with both hands.

“Woo… woo…”

Some of the windows that were closed before were blown open again. The wind was stronger, and carried more disturbances than before. Many of the winds seemed to turn and twist into some of the lampshades, causing the flames to shake violently.

The lights flickered in the Zhongtai Building. Some guests were disturbed by this strange change. The Gao family ordered the servants to close the windows, but found that many windows could not be closed.

The wind was getting stronger and stronger, and the candles in the lampshades were extinguished one after another.

“Ah…”

“What’s going on!”

“Master…”

Everyone panicked, but Wang Li in the center of the hall staggered and felt slightly dazed…

But in the next moment, all the winds stopped. The windows that could not be closed before suddenly lost their resistance, causing several of the servants who were trying to close them to stumble into the windows.

The woman in white looked around and looked out of the window in astonishment. She waved her hands but found that there was no reaction around her.

“Heh…”

Ji Yuan chuckled lightly, and lifted his index finger off the table. The characters “still winds”4 remained on the edge of his cup in the form of rice wine stains.

神女 (shénnǚ): literally translates to “goddess.” However, be aware that sometimes “goddess” in Chinese is used to refer to beautiful women. In this case, it’s an actual goddess

It isn’t clear here whether it refers to an emperor’s dynasty or just the name for a period of time

Also known as “Cantonese poached chicken”

定 (dìng): to fix/to set/to coagulate, 风 (fēng): wind

Chapter 258: One Must Be Realistic

[T/n: note on chapter title]1

Judging from the appearance of this woman in white, she was likely a ghost cultivator who had gone astray. She lacked understanding and control over her wind-control techniques, and her cultivation level was not very high. It was impossible for her to play tricks by calling the wind to mess with people in front of Ji Yuan.

The woman was nervous for a while, but she did not find anything else unusual, so she felt relieved.

As soon as the wind calmed down, the guests who had previously been disturbed settled down. The waiters of the restaurant and the servants of the Gao family quickly light the lamps that had been extinguished. Some people checked the wooden pins on the windows, wondering how they could have been blown open by the wind just now.

From sunset up until now, they had finished the entire story, and the dishes had almost all been eaten. This banquet could truly be regarded as a feast, both for the guests and for the hosts.

The guests said goodbye and left one after another. However, it was not yet time to clear the tables. The sound of the zithers and harps continued throughout the building, and people were still exchanging cups and drinking. Those who had stayed behind were all wine-lovers. The story just now was fascinating, so they didn’t drink much before, as they didn’t want to get drunk and miss it. Now, of course, they naturally planned to stay drunk.

Wang Li rubbed his dizzy forehead. He was the one who was hit hardest by that cold wind just now, so he was now sober.

“Mr. Wang, the patriarch ordered to settle your payment, please come with me.”

A steward of the Gao family came to Wang Li’s side and led the storyteller, who had completely cleared up his table, to collect his payment.

“Okay, let’s go!”

Wang Li was overjoyed when he heard about the money. He hurriedly picked up his belongings and went downstairs with the steward.

Ji Yuan saw that the woman in white also stood up in a hurry, and followed Wang Li downstairs. He drank the wine in the glass in front of him, wiped off the wine-stained handwriting on the table, and stood up.

However, Ji Yuan did not go downstairs directly, but instead walked over to the table where the patriarch of the Gao family sat, and bowed with cupped hands to the Young Master Gao, who was still accompanying him.

“Young Master Gao, thank you for your hospitality. I will take my leave first, as I have something else to do. Please give my regards to Patriarch Gao!”

After all, Mr. Gao was very old, so he couldn’t stay up late. Additionally, he had drank a lot of wine because he was so happy, so he had returned to his house to rest. Either way, most of the elders of the same seniority had already gone back.

Young Master Gao didn’t know Ji Yuan at all, but he felt that he couldn’t simply dismiss his request to “give my regards to Patriarch Gao” out of hand. Besides, Ji Yuan was indeed quite elegant, so he hurriedly stood up and returned the bow.

“Okay, Master2 Ji. Take care!”

Ji Yuan, who had been about to turn around, instantly became happy when he heard the words “Master Ji.”

“Ha, Master Ji? Hahahaha…Interesting, interesting…”

Although this Young Master Gao was well-educated, his habits were not those of a scholar; because most of the Gao family’s friends were businessmen, the people who had attended this banquet were all wealthy merchants.

Since he didn’t know Ji Yuan, Young Master Gao naturally assumed that he was an individual from a wealthy family with ties to his father, so he called him Master Ji out of habit.

Ji Yuan smiled and shook his head.

“Young Master, now that you have graduated from your schooling, you will most likely go to Wan Province to serve as an official. The journey is quite long, and the climate is slightly different. You need to prepare well. Before leaving your hometown, don’t forget to pray at your ancestors’ temple and burn incense, and bring a handful of soil from your hometown with you.”

“Wan Province?”

Young Master Gao thought for a moment, then understood the reason in an instant. From the end of the previous year up to the beginning of this last year, the “Bloody Silk Incident” had shaken the court and the general public. There were many rumors circulating in You Province, but in the end it was too far away, so they only knew that a lot of people were killed, all of whom were corrupt officials. They didn’t know exactly how serious it was. From what Mr. Ji was saying, it seemed as though there were still a lot of vacancies in the official positions there?

‘Wan Province is a good place! It’s also a great opportunity to develop my ambitions! ’

“Thank you, Mr. Ji, for your advice!”

Young Master Gao solemnly bowed again. This time, he did not call him Master.

Ji Yuan nodded, then turned to leave. Young Master Gao watched him go downstairs, then sat down to continue drinking.

I couldn’t help but think that among all the people his father knew, there actually was one who didn’t smell like copper coins. He felt as though he had met a celebrity.3 He had to go back and ask his father. It would be best if he could invite this Mr. Ji to his house and be able to chat with him.

Downstairs, the butler of the Gao family borrowed the Zhongtai Building’s silver scale to weigh the silver taels in front of Wang Li. There were two ingots of silver in total, one of five taels and the other of one tael.

“The weight of the silver is exactly right. Please take it, Mr. Wang. Five taels is your remuneration. This extra tael is the additional reward that the patriarch ordered to give you.”

Wang Li bowed solemnly.

“Thank you!”

Only then did he take the money. His expression grew even more joyful. He said goodbye and then left Zhongtai Building after thanking them again and again.

After Wang Li left, the woman in white followed him out. As she passed by the butler of the Gao family, the latter suddenly shivered.

“Hooh… it’s cold tonight…”

As soon as he turned around, he saw Ji Yuan coming down, and he smiled and nodded again. Ji Yuan smiled back and strode out of Zhongtai Building.

Although the night watchman had not yet come around to ring the gong of the third watch, it was almost the end of the Hai hour.4 Most residents of the city were already soundly asleep, and the streets were empty.

After Wang Li walked out of Zhongtai Building, he hurriedly headed south.

“Whooo… whoooo…”

The night wind blew, making his body grow cold. Wang Li tightened his clothes around him and his steps quickened.

When he came to a fork in the street, he hesitated for a while. He could either choose to go home, or go in another direction. In the end, he did not choose to go home, and instead turned to the west.

“Mr. Wang, Mr. Wang, please wait!”

A cold and pleasant voice came from behind him. Wang Li turned around doubtfully,5 and found a woman in white fluttering robes standing behind him.

Wang Li looked around, but it seemed that no one else was following him.

“Are you calling for me, young lady?”

The woman bowed to Wang Li and smiled.

“I heard that Mr. Wang’s “White Deer’s Fate” is filled with sincere emotion. It is a blessing to hear the last three chapters today.”

She heard it before?

Wang Li frowned. He had never spoken of “The White Deer’s Fate” in Chengsu Prefecture. How could she have heard of it before? Could it be that this woman had heard his story in other places?

“Oh, thank you for your praise, young lady. It’s late at night, and it’s too careless for a young lady to walk on the streets alone. You should head home quickly.”

“Mr. Wang, you are right. I am very afraid to walk alone at night. I wonder if Mr. Wang could send this little girl home?”

“This… a man and a woman…”

“Does Mr. Wang want this little girl to walk alone?”

The woman in white asked again. Seeing that Wang Li was still hesitating, she moved closer to Wang Li and whispered something.

“Mr. Wang, please follow me. I can help you see Duan Muwan again.”

“Wan’er!? You know her? Let’s go, let’s go!”

Wang Li no longer hesitated and left with the woman. Behind him, Ji Yuan, who was still following them, frowned and thought for a moment.

‘Who is Duan Muwan? This woman in white’s divine incense power is not very stable, and I don’t know where she came from. ’

Ji Yuan kept, each footstep as though the ground were shrinking, and followed Wang Li and the woman comfortably.

Wang Li, as an ordinary man, didn’t notice, but Ji Yuan could see it clearly in this moment. Because they were being led by the woman in white, the pair’s walking speed was unusually fast, almost faster than an ordinary person’s running pace.

In the night wind, the three of them walked farther and farther away, before finally arriving at the city wall. The woman dragged Wang Li to walk on the city wall and, as though they were walking through space, they quickly stepped out of the city and left Chengsu Prefecture’s capital.

Wang Li was caught in a confusion technique, and did not realize his current situation at al. He simply thought that he was still walking along the city streets with the woman.

Ji Yuan was as light as a swallow. He leapt up and dropped down the city wall, still following the two of them. He wanted to see what this goddess was up to.

Soon, the two of them had already walked ten li away from the city. Only then did they slow don. It seemed as though they didn’t have any specific destination.

“Mr. Wang, I have something I don’t quite understand, and I wanted to ask you.”

“Did Wan’er ask you to ask? Miss, please go ahead.”

Wang Li frequently looked into the distance, believing that someone would be waiting there.

The woman in white turned around with a cold expression and stared into Wang Li’s eyes.

“Mr. Wang, where did you get the story of “The White Deer’s Fate”? Was the Lady White Deer in the story truly imprisoned in the underworld, and tortured year after year?”

“I don’t know, I’ve never seen her. And Wan’er? Where is Wan’er?”

Wang Li seemed absent-minded, still bewitched.

“Wang Li! I’m asking you a question I’ve been looking for you for so long, don’t play dumb here!”

“Ah? Miss, you have been looking for me for a long time? Stop joking, where is Wan’er?”

The woman in white sneered and swung an arm at Wang Li. The latter stumbled and fell to the ground, rubbing his stinging forehead, and woke up.

“What… where is this? Am I… am I dreaming?”

It was wilderness as far as his eyes could see. There was no trace of the streets and buildings of the city.

“Wang Li, tell me how you know the story of “The White Deer’s Fate.” How could you, a mere mortal, know about such things, and… and… in such detail…”

Wang Li was rubbing his arms in this moment, looking a little flustered. He had fallen at an awkward angle just now, and it hurt a lot. This likely was not a dream; it might be that he had encountered a demon.

“Auntie… lady… I said before I started telling that story that it was imparted upon me by a god in a dream. I slightly modified it and polished it to bring the story to life.”

“A god? Haha, imparted by a god in a dream? What deity would specifically entrust you with the story of a monster falling in love?”

Wang Li gulped.

“Actually, I received the three words “White Deer’s Fate” written by that god, and when I touched them, a scene appeared in my mind, and then I fell into a dream…”

The woman was stunned for a moment. To convey knowledge through objects?

In that case, it was a little more credible.

Thinking of this, the woman grew furious.

“So, Lady White Deer is truly suffering from being whipped in the underworld just to accompany her husband? That bastard Zhou Niansheng actually dragged her into the underworld with him. The whipping of the underworld will destroy her soul!”

There was a cold blue light in the eyes of the woman opposite. Her pale face approached Wang Li’s, and one hand grabbed Wang Li’s neck. Her nails were very long.

“Ghost… gho… gho… ghost…”

Wang Li was so frightened that his face turned pale, his legs became weak, and his survival instinct made him tremble to defend himself:

“No, no… That god… the outcome imparted by the gods is… it was more favorable… Lady White Deer, although she went to the underworld… but… the land gods and the immortal guaranteed that… the underworld did not make things difficult for her. She only received one lashing every year…”6

The grim-faced woman was visibly stunned.

“Didn’t your story say that the Land God who pleaded for mercy was turned away by the City God, and the White Deer was whipped all day long every year on Zhou Niansheng’s birthday?”

“This… it’s just… it’s just a slightly embellished creation… I swear to the heavens that I would never lie to you!”

A dim light flickered in the woman’s eyes, as if to see whether Wang Li was lying or not, but the latter’s face was pale, and he dared not look at her.

“How dare you lie to me!”

In a rage, woman’s fingernails suddenly grew longer, clawing at one of Wang Li’s eyes.

“Freeze.”7

At the instant of Ji Yuan’s command, the blue nails were only an inch away from Wang Li’s left eye. The latter was so scared that he was paralyzed, his breathing shaky.

Chapter 259: Out Of Your League

[T/n: note on chapter title]1

Although Wang Li was so frightened that his heart almost skipped a beat, he still had his wits about him, understood that a master must have saved him.

The woman in front of him had completely lost any expression, not even moving her eyes. Even so, Wang Li didn’t dare to look at her.

He wanted to turn his head, but although the terrifying woman in front of him was motionless, her left hand was still clamped around his neck, making it impossible for Wang Li to break free or move. He could only look to the side in his peripheral vision.

It was dark, and he couldn’t see clearly. He could only make out a gentleman in a green robe, slowly approaching.

Ji Yuan walked over to the pair. He had just used a temporary immobilization method, one that he hadn’t prepared in advance. In addition, the other party was quite skilled with regards to incense power and faith. To some extent, although it was a body-freezing technique, he couldn’t freeze her for too long.

So Ji Yuan simply waved his arm at the woman, and the latter was bounced two or three zhang away. He also took the opportunity to release the immobilization technique, allowing her to regain control of her body.

“Ouch…haah…haah…”

Because the woman lost her grip, Wang Li yelped and fell onto the barren ground with weak legs. He wanted to struggle to stand up, but his legs were too weak, so he could only continuously bow to Ji Yuan from his position on the ground.

“Thank you for saving my life, thank you for saving my life!”

Ji Yuan glanced calmly at Wang Li, and then at the woman in white. Just him standing there casually put the latter on high alert, and she didn’t even dare to escape.

This newcomer must be an orthodox immortal. The woman was very sure of this. Instead of the fierce expression from just now, she put away her nails, smoothed out her face, and stared at Ji Yuan seriously.

“I didn’t necessarily save your life. She probably wouldn’t dare to kill you directly, but what I am curious about is…”

The first half of Ji Yuan’s sentence was to Wang Li, while the second half was to the woman in white.

“Do you have a deep friendship with the White Deer, or do you simply have a deep hatred for Wang Li?”

“Sure enough, there was a master present. No wonder that technique in the restaurant earlier failed. Hmph, is this immortal master relying on his spiritual powers to bully this little girl?”

The woman in white asked Ji Yuan in a cold voice. In truth, she was far from as calm as she appeared on the surface. The spiritual technique just now was too mysterious, she had never heard of anything like it before.

This sentence made Ji Yuan laugh.

“Interesting, I froze you with my power, so you say it is natural for you to come find this storyteller?”

However, the woman was not stumped by the question. After thinking quickly, she had already come up with an excuse, and pointed directly at Wang Li as she answered.

“I have an answer to the two questions the Immortal asked. First, Sister Bai Ruo was kind to me and helped me avenge a life and death debt. Naturally, this is a deep kindness offered to me.”

“Secondly, regardless of whether or not Wang Li deceived me about the ending, he even made up a story about me to belittle me in the book. The more it spreads, the greater the impact it will have on me. In light of this, taking out his eye is already being lenient!”

Ji Yuan looked at Wang Li in surprise and then back at the woman.

“He made up a story about you?”

Then his mind moved as if sensing something.

“Are you the ghost in the second chapter of “The White Deer’s Fate”?”

Ji Yuan asked in surprise. The woman in white was annoyed, but did not dare to attack Ji Yuan. She could only watch Wang Li and grit her teeth.

“That is correct, Immortal. This little girl is that evil ghost who eats people, sucking out blood and marrow without even leaving the bones!”

Indeed, it was no coincidence.

Even Ji Yuan thought that in that chapter about the White Deer’s past, the ghost should have already exhausted its yin life, and its earthly soul returned to the earth, and heavenly soul returned to the sky.2

Unexpectedly, not only was it still around, it was also walking a Divine Path.

Wang Li was a little dazed when he heard this. The so-called artistic freedom was a common technique in storytelling. Take the famous “Legend of General Huang,” for example. In real life, General Huang was a flawed individual with his own shortcomings, but in the legend, he was the perfect image of heroic loyalty.

In order to make the story more exciting, with more twists and turns, Wang Li naturally added a lot of subjective story content in the middle of “The White Deer’s Fate.” The biggest villains in the second chapter were the “evil ghost” and “bad teammates and the confused experts.”

“In the Divine Path of cultivation, the power of faith and will is important, as are the thoughts, hearts, and words of human beings. There is no way to avoid it, and it will indeed have an impact. If “The White Deer’s Fate” becomes more popular in the future, it will indeed be regarded as…”

Ji Yuan thought about it for a while, before finally speaking.

“It would be enough to render you ‘disgusting.’”

After all, most of Wang Li’s stories did not name names, it would be a bit too far to claim that it would completely cut off her cultivation path, but it nonetheless had an impact, and that indeed would render her nothing more than a disgusting ghost.

“So, you came from Yan Province? It’s no wonder your incense power is unstable. It must have been a long time since you first came to this realm, right?”

The Divine Path of the Land God was not the same as true cultivation. The longer one was outside of their established boundary, the more incense power and spiritual energy would b consumed, and there was no way to replenish it. It was a constant state of expenditure, rather than growth. Moreover, one’s strength and spiritual power would greatly decrease, as one would no longer be in the jurisdiction of their own land.

‘She is an individual who values friendship and loyalty. She is probably concerned about the White Deer.’

Ji Yuan came to this conclusion because the woman did not directly accuse Wang Li of harming her. Instead, she eagerly inquired about the White Deer’s current situation. It was only when she encountered a master that she brought up the cause and effect to justify her “murder.”

“I did indeed come from You Province after hearing about it from merchants in the capital, but how did the Immortal know that I was from Yan Province? “The White Deer’s Fate” did not state it. Did the Immortal divine it?”

The woman in white had greatly relaxed. Judging from the current situation, this immortal who had just arrived was reasonable, and likely wouldn’t make things too difficult for her.

Ji Yuan shook his head with a smile. He didn’t intend to hide anything. He answered her question with an irrelevant comment to Wang Li, who was lying on the ground.

“Back then, in the rented room in the corner alley off of Yongning Lane, in the capital, it was I who wrote those three words, ‘White Deer’s Fate,’ on your desk.”

Wang Li’s eyes suddenly widened, and he pointed at Ji Yuan, speechless.

“You… you… you… you are the immortal who helped the White Deer descend to the underworld, and you are the old immortal that the Lady White Deer carried on her back!”

The goddess in white was stunned for a moment, but them she realized what this relationship meant. She understood the implications better than Wang Li, and couldn’t help but utter:

“Immortal Master, you… Are you Elder Sister Bai Ruo’s master?”

Ji Yuan opened his mouth, but realized that it was better for this kind of white lie not to be clarified, so he could only sigh.

“Nominally, I suppose.”

His sigh and tone, from the perspectives of Wang Li and the woman in white, became a sigh of pity for his disciple.

. . .

The grass and trees in the wilderness were sparse, and the night wind blew over the landscape and created a thin mist. After the initial tense confrontation, Wang Li and the woman in white finally breathed a sigh of relief.

In particular, when they learned that those first three words, “White Deer’s Fate,” were written by Ji Yuan, both Wang Li and the goddess in white, named Zhang Rui, were all excited.

The former felt that Ji Yuan had not only saved his life, but was also a god, while the latter knew the true condition of the White Deer was thanks to him.

Both Wang Li and even Zhang Rui were willing to believe Ji Yuan.

Ji Yuan’s words were naturally different from Wang Li’s previous guilty conscience and fear. He had a convincing bearing, and some details were consistent. Moreover, in the eyes of the woman in white, a cultivator of this level of Taoism would not bother to lie around such a big incident.

As for the matter of the woman in white being harmed in her cultivation, Wang Li was no fool. He promised that he would definitely modify the plot, and so Zhang Rui let Wang Li go for Ji Yuan’s sake.

At this moment, it was already in the latter part of the midnight hour. Wang Li, Ji Yuan, and Zhang Rui were walking together back towards Chengsu Prefecture’s capital. They were not heading back towards Wang Li’s home, but rather to the southwest of the city, which was where Wang Li wanted to go before.

As the three walked, Ji Yuan was in the middle, with Wang Li and Zhang Rui, the woman in white, on the left and right respectively.

“Duan Muwan is a famous prostitute in You Province. She is proficient in music, chess, calligraphy, and painting.3 She can also see into people’s hearts, and has fascinated many rich and powerful men. She is called Hongxiu, the Red Beauty.4 Hm, in truth, the current Hongxiu is no longer a real human, but rather a fox spirit pretending to be a fake.”

“Huh?”

Ji Yuan frowned and glanced at Zhang Rui, waiting for her next words. To the side, Wang Li looked incredulous.

“How is that possible? I just saw Wan’er two days ago…she, she is definitely human!”

“Hey, didn’t this little girl look human when I got you lost just now?”

Zhang Rui mocked Wang Li, pretending to be a little weak girl, which immediately rendered the latter speechless. Then Zhang Rui turned back to Ji Yuan and said solemnly:

“I have been staying in Chengsu Prefecture for only a short time. I originally planned to wait for the storyteller at the large performance boat5 tonight, but I accidentally discovered Red Beauty’s true origins. There have been several fox spirits on the mountainside in the land under my jurisdiction. I cannot mistake that smell.”

Ji Yuan narrowed his eyes.

“Has the underworld not discovered it, or have they ignored it?”

“The underworld probably doesn’t know yet. Hongxiu is not from Chengsu Prefecture, and she is not dead. The performance boat is floating on the Sushui River, which belongs to the water god’s territory. Plus, she is acting carefully, so she is even more concealed.”

Ji Yuan glanced at Wang Li out of the corner of his eye with a thoughtful look on his face.

“Hongxiu… that sounds familiar…”

That’s right, couldn’t she be that woman that the young master of the Xiao family was in love with on that tower ship back then?6

一枝独秀 (yīzhīdúxiù): lit. “only one branch of the tree is thriving,” idiom means to be outstanding/in a league of your own

From Taoist philosophy — humans are thought to possess multiple souls, the heavenly (Hun 魂) and earthly (Po 魄) souls. The Hun is associated with yang, and is the spiritual aspect, thought to ascend after death. The Po is associated with yin, the corporeal/instinctual aspect, and believed to return to the earth.

The four most important arts that are used to judge the level of a woman’s education in Ancient China

Previously left as “Hongxiu,” but it’s a title meaning “Red Beauty.” I have chosen to leave it as Hongxiu unless it’s explicitly used as a title rather than a name, just for continuity’s purpose.

大 (dài): big, 秀 (xiù): performance/elegant/refined, 船 (chuán): boat/ship. These tend to be richly decorated boats used for expensive performances or banquets, kind of like floating theaters.

Chapter 260: Very Simple

The three continued walking in the night through the silent city, heading southwest.

Ji Yuan didn’t mention the option of going to the City God Temple or anything like that. Zhang Rui already knew that she would have to go there in person to see the situation.

For this kind of matter, Ji Yuan would not look to the ghosts and gods of the underworld. Firstly, it would be easy to stumble into jurisdiction disputes, and secondly, it would be troublesome. It was better to keep these solutions simple and crude.

Of the three of them, Ji Yuan was curious and had his own speculations, Wang Li was frowning, and Zhang Rui was the most relaxed. Mr. Ji had helped her by writing an Edict, which was equivalent to helping her temporarily condense her spiritual power, so she didn’t have to worry like before.

One peculiar aspect of Chengsu Prefecture’s capital was that the southern edge of the city actually did not have a wall, but instead directly bordered the Sushui River. As a large waterway, the width of this section of the river was dozens of zhang wide. This construction decision created a natural large port in the southwestern section of the city.

This large port was not only the water transportation hub of Chengsu Prefecture, but also one of the prefecture’s most prosperous locations. It was also a popular romantic spot. Of course, it was far from large compared to other constructions outside of Chengsu Prefecture.

The large performance boat that Zhang Rui mentioned, the Daxiu Ship,1 was docked here. It was one of the more famous brothel ships in Chengsu Prefecture, and their red show2 was even famous throughout You Province. Even some rich sons and young masters in the capital prefecture would visit due to its reputation.

As opposed to the silence of other parts of the city, there were still pedestrians in the parts of the city around the Sushui Pier at this current moment. Of course, it was obvious what their destination was, to be walking around outside at this hour and coming to such a place. Some of them had even secretly snuck out of their houses.

The Daxiu Ship was one destination; in fact, in addition to the tower ship, there was also a larger brothel on land next to it, called the Daxiu Building.3 The real Daxiu Ship was parked in a concave curve in the river behind the building. The buildings to either side belonged to the Daxiu Building, essentially forming a small harbor to surround the performance boat. Only after entering the brothel and walknig all the way to the back of the building could you enter the Daxiu Ship.

Before Ji Yuan and the others arrived, the sounds of mixed laughter, teasing, and screaming had already reached Ji Yuan’s ears. Soon, the complex smell of rouge and powder also rushed into his nose.

There was nothing to be embarrassed about; Ji Yuan led Wang Li directly into the brothel. Although Zhang Rui also followed, she had obviously cast a concealment technique, so that no one other than Ji Yuan and Wang Li could see her.

“Mr. Zhang, you are here quite late today?”

“Official Zhao, have a good time!”

“Come on, gentlemen, come on in…”

Some women outside the brothel were pulling customers. At this hour, some people who passed by outside would directly go in. There was no need to pretend this late. Even if some people pretended to pass by, they would half-push and half-be-pushed into the building after being called.

“Hey~! Mr. Wang, you are here again~ Come in quickly! Oh, who is this gentleman, such a charming demeanor!”

The three of them got closer, and Wang Li was immediately recognized by the girls outside. Someone immediately came over and took his hand to lead him inside.

“Uh, heh… Mr. Ji, I… I don’t come here that often…”

The brothel girls were quite discerning. They had a rough understanding of who could be pulled intimately, and whom it would be better not to touch too casually.

For example, Wang Li was pulled in quite affectionately, and they even rubbed against him with their bodies, but the others did not dare to do so with Ji Yuan, despite how many girls were watching him, and some who were even moved by his appearance.

But Ji Yuan was just standing there, and no one dared to casually pull him in.

“Hmph, not that often? Who would believe that? Mr. Ji, look, this guy can’t even be honest!”

Wang Li’s face flushed red, and he didn’t dare to look at Ji Yuan’s expression. This was the immortal of his dream, and he didn’t want to consider what he might think of him.

Ji Yuan had never faced this kind of situation in his previous life. If it had been the Ji Yuan of before, he would probably have blushed heavily, but at this moment, there was no particular emotion on his face, and his mood was relatively indifferent. Of course, there was still a trace of nervousness in his heart, but he was neither excited nor disgusted by the scene.

In the previous world, some prostitutes might have just been trying to make easy money,4 but in this world, all the girls in the brothels were were basically either sold to a brothel as children, or had become prostitutes due to some disaster. The social and environmental pressure of this industry was enough to crush people. Aside from a lucky few, there was almost no chance of these women being able to start anew in their lives.

“Mr. Wang, why don’t you come in? Sir, would you like to come in and rest for a while? It’s very windy and cold outside!”

These girls could see that Wang Li was looking at Ji Yuan with an embarrassed look on his face.

Ji Yuan nodded immediately.

“Mr. Wang, let’s go in.”

The women to the side were overjoyed, and immediately several of them rushed to hold Ji Yuan’s hand.

“Buzz…”

The Green Vine Sword behind Ji Yuan suddenly rang out, and a burst of suppressed sword intent filled Ji Yuan’s body, radiating over half a zhang away.

Both Wang Li and some of the brothel girls heard a ringing in their ears, felt extremely flustered. Many people even subconsciously took a few steps back in a panic.

“They are just unfortunate people who have no choice.”

Ji Yuan murmured in a low voice, and the terrifying feeling that made people’s hearts race disappeared instantly.

Zhang Rui retreated a dozen feet in horror, looking at Ji Yuan’s position from a distance, not daring to approach. She could sense it more clearly than ordinary mortals.

At that very moment, it was as if she could see an illusion of endless bright silver light filling her entire perception. It carried an indescribable sharpness, as if she were an ordinary person feeling the piercing sensation of a knife, scraping her bone with icy wind.

At this moment, Zhang Rui suddenly remembered that in the original version of “The White Deer’s Fate,” there was an immortal sword hanging around the “old immortal.”

‘That means…’

The goddess in white subconsciously glanced around Ji Yuan and in the sky above him. Just because she couldn’t see it didn’t mean it wasn’t there. It was normal for someone with a lower level of cultivation to not be able to see the immortal weapon.

While Zhang Rui was stunned, Ji Yuan and Wang Li had already entered the Daxiu Building. However, as opposed to the other guests, who were being hugged and tugged as they entered, the girls around the two of them only dared to lead them from the front at a distance, saying a few awkward words in conversation, and they didn’t dare touch their bodies at all.

Shaking her head, Zhang Rui gritted her teeth and followed them inside.

Inside the Daxiu Building, the busy Madam was arranging for several guests to be brought upstairs. When she turned around, she spotted Ji Yuan and Wang Li coming in, but her eyes only stopped on Wang Li for a moment before focusing directly on Ji Yuan. At first glance, she could tell that this newcomer might be of extraordinary status.

“Oh, Mr. Wang, you are here to do business again. Unfortunately, Miss Hongxiu still has customers today… And who might this be…”

The Madam greeted him with a smile, fanning herself with a round fan, and glared at the girls next to him. These girls were too ignorant, and couldn’t tell who should be more warmly entertained.

“Sir, are you Mr. Wang’s friend?”

The Madam stood beside Ji Yuan with a smile, and pretended to look him over casually. Although she couldn’t see any expensive accessories on his body, and his clothes were simple, he still felt extraordinary.

In particular, when she took a closer look at the seemingly ordinary black jade hairpin, it resembled transparent glass under the light, attracting people’s eyes.

‘Extremely rare, extremely precious! What a big fish!’5

When Wang Li heard the Madam’s words, he subconsciously showed some slight panic.

“Oh, madam,6 don’t speak nonsense. How could I be qualified to be Mr. Ji’s friend… Sir is… uh, my elder, yes, he is of the honored generation!”

Although Wang Li was just a storyteller, he was experienced in the ways of the world. Some young masters who appeared to be distinguished were in fact not so esteemed in Wang Li’s eyes. Occasionally, when he drank too much, he would inadvertently reveal this attitude.

Seeing Wang Li’s cautious reaction at this moment, the Madam smiled like a blooming chrysanthemum. She fanned the fragrance of rouge and powder towards Ji Yuan with two movements, bowed, and offered a thousand blessings.7

“Hahahaha… Mr. Wang must be joking. Sir, why don’t come to the performance boat~ The girls in the performance building are not worthy of you!”

The smell of excessive powder made Ji Yuan feel a little uncomfortable. The Green Vine Sword behind him did not ring out or emit any sword intent, but in the spiritual text on the scabbard, the character “concealed” had actually faded. If someone who was aware of this could see it, they would probably break out into a cold sweat.

Ji Yuan resisted the urge to blow away the thick and choking smell of powder with a wind technique, and calmly asked the Madam.

“I wonder if Miss Hongxiu is free?”

“Uh…this…Sir, Miss Hongxiu is playing the zither for Elder Official Liu. Elder Official Liu is the uncle of the prefect of Chengsu Prefecture. Sir, he is an official, we must afford him some respect, so we should…”

“Tsk…”

Listening to the Madam’s chattering explanation, Wang Li couldn’t help but sneer. How could the prefect’s uncle compare to Mr. Ji?

As soon as this sound came out, he watched Ji Yuan turn to look at him with indifference. He was so scared that his face turned pale, and he didn’t dare react any more.

The Madam’s eyes lit up, and her heart trembled slightly. What a man, he didn’t even care about the prefect, he was even better than she imagined!

“Uh, hahaha… How about the two of you head to the Daxiu Ship first to rest and have some tea?”

The Madam smiled, and continued considering her approach in her heart. This guest definitely had to stay.

“All right.”

Ji Yuan smiled for the first time since had entered this brothel, and walked towards the back of the building. A faint demonic aura was floating out from the back of the brothel, and Ji Yuan couldn’t smell any traces of powder to cover it up.

Just as Wang Li was about to catch up, he found himself being held back by the Madam, who laned close to his ear and asked in a low voice.

“Mr. Wang, Mr. Wang, what’s the background of that gentleman? I promise not to tell anyone. Please give me a definite answer. If his identity is important enough, Miss Hongxiu will…”

Wang Li’s eyes lit up, and he looked at Ji Yuan, who was almost ten paces away in front, and leaned closer to the Madam’s ear.

“If I tell you about his identity, it would scare you to death. Don’t even mention the prefect’s uncle, even the prefect himself cannot compare! As for who he is… I cannot say…”

“Oh oh oh… I understand…!”

The madam gulped and nodded with a look of astonishment. She understood that the identity of this gentleman was not so simple. She looked back, she found that the man in question had already gone far away, and hurriedly turned around to pursue him.

Zhang Rui carefully stepped closer to Ji Yuan and asked in a low voice.

“Mr. Ji, can I help you drive that vixen out?”

Ji Yuan shook his head.

“Your skills are not good enough. That fox demon is not so simple; it is not just a fox skilled in deceit, it might be a fox demon who has already achieved its transformation, or perhaps even more powerful than that! There are too many people here…”

Again, this naming was not in the raws, but I added to clarify what I will be calling the performance boat from here on. 大秀 (dà xiù) literally means “big beauty” or “big show”

Red, as in red light district, being the color of the lanterns used to demarcate the brothel/pleasure districts. “Red show” would mean that it’s the prostitutes performing. However, this is not necessarily sexual in nature, as this show might consist of playing musical instruments or dancing.

Same characters as previously mentioned for “performance boat,” except change the single character 船 (chuán) “boat” to 楼 (lóu) “building”
